The Buddha’s lifespan was forty-two thousand kalpas, and the lifespans of the Bodhisattvas were also equal. That land had no women, hells, hungry ghosts, animals, asuras, or tribulations; the ground was flat as the palm of a hand, made of lapis lazuli, adorned with jewel trees, covered with jewel curtains, draped with jewel flower banners; jewel vases and censers pervaded the realm; seven-jeweled platforms stood, one tree to each platform, the trees separated from the platforms by the distance of an arrow’s flight.

Beneath all these jewel trees, Bodhisattvas and Śrāvakas sat. Upon each jewel platform, tens of billions of devas made heavenly music, singing praises to the Buddha as offerings. At that time, that Buddha expounded the Lotus Sūtra for Sarvasattvapriyadarśana Bodhisattva, the assembly of Bodhisattvas, and the assembly of Śrāvakas.

This Sarvasattvapriyadarśana Bodhisattva delighted in practicing austerities. Within the Dharma of Candrasūryavimalaprabhāsaśrī Buddha, he vigorously practiced walking meditation, single-mindedly seeking Buddhahood; fulfilling twelve thousand years, he attained the samādhi of manifesting all physical bodies. Having attained this samādhi, his heart rejoiced greatly, thinking: ‘My attaining the samādhi of manifesting all physical bodies is entirely through the power of hearing the Lotus Sūtra; I should now make offerings to Candrasūryavimalaprabhāsaśrī Buddha and the Lotus Sūtra.’

Immediately he entered this samādhi, and from open space rained down māndārava flowers, mahā-māndārava flowers, and fine powdered hard black sandalwood, filling open space, descending like clouds; he also rained the sandalwood incense of this shore of the ocean—six grains of this incense being worth the Sahā world—to make offerings to the Buddha.

Having made this offering, he arose from samādhi and thought to himself: ‘Although I have made offerings to the Buddha through spiritual powers, it is not equal to offering my own body.’

Immediately he ingested various fragrances—sandalwood, frankincense, turuṣka, priyaṅgu, aloeswood, and resin incense—and drank the scented oil of campaka flowers. Fulfilling twelve hundred years, he anointed his body with scented oil, and before Candrasūryavimalaprabhāsaśrī Buddha, wrapped his body in heavenly jewel robes, poured scented oil over himself, and through the power of spiritual vows burned his own body. Its light universally illuminated worlds equal to the sands of eighty billion Ganges rivers.

The Buddhas within them simultaneously praised: ‘Excellent, excellent! Good son! This is genuine vigor; this is named making offerings of the genuine Dharma to the Tathāgata. If with flowers, incense, necklaces, burning incense, powdered incense, paste incense, heavenly silk banners, canopies, and the sandalwood incense of this shore of the ocean, one were to make offerings with such various things, it could not equal this; even if giving countries, cities, wives, and children, it could not equal this. Good son! This is named the foremost giving; among all givings, it is supreme and unexcelled, because one makes offerings of the Dharma to the Tathāgatas!’ Having spoken these words, they became silent.

His body burned with fire for twelve hundred years; only after this did his body become exhausted.

Sarvasattvapriyadarśana Bodhisattva, having made such a Dharma offering, after his life ended was reborn in the land of Candrasūryavimalaprabhāsaśrī Buddha, in the home of King Pure Virtue, sitting cross-legged, suddenly born by transformation. Immediately he spoke gāthās for his father:

Great King, you should now know:

Practicing walking meditation in that place,

Immediately I attained

The samādhi of manifesting all bodies.

Diligently practicing great vigor,

I abandoned the body I cherished,

Making offerings to the World-Honored One,

To seek unexcelled wisdom.

Having spoken these gāthās, he addressed his father: ‘Candrasūryavimalaprabhāsaśrī Buddha is even now present. Earlier, having made offerings to the Buddha, I attained the dhāraṇī of understanding the languages of all wandering beings, and further heard this Lotus Sūtra in hundreds of thousands of millions of inconceivable numbers of kaṅkaras, bimbaras, and akṣobhyas of verses. Great King! I should now return to make offerings to this Buddha.’

Having spoken thus, he sat upon a seven-jeweled platform, ascended into open space to the height of seven tāla trees, went to where the Buddha was, bowed with his head touching the Buddha’s feet, joined his ten fingers, and praised the Buddha in gāthās:

Your countenance is extraordinarily wondrous,

Your light illuminates the ten directions;

I recently made offerings to you,

And now return in person to behold you.

Then Sarvasattvapriyadarśana Bodhisattva, having spoken these gāthās, addressed the Buddha: ‘World-Honored One! The World-Honored One is still in the world!’

At that time, Candrasūryavimalaprabhāsaśrī Buddha addressed Sarvasattvapriyadarśana Bodhisattva: ‘Good son! The time of my nirvāṇa has arrived, the time of extinction is here; you may arrange a seat; tonight I will enter parinirvāṇa.’

He further commanded Sarvasattvapriyadarśana Bodhisattva: ‘Good son! I entrust the Buddha-Dharma to you and all the Bodhisattva great disciples, along with the Dharma of anuttarasamyaksambodhi; and I entrust to you the seven-jeweled worlds of the great three-thousand-fold worlds, with all their jewel trees, jewel platforms, and attendant devas. After my extinction, whatever relics there are I also entrust to you; you should cause them to be distributed, widely setting forth offerings, erecting several thousand stūpas.’

In this manner Candrasūryavimalaprabhāsaśrī Buddha commanded Sarvasattvapriyadarśana Bodhisattva, and in the latter part of the night entered nirvāṇa.

Then Sarvasattvapriyadarśana Bodhisattva, seeing the Buddha pass into extinction, grieved, lamented, and longed for the Buddha. Immediately with sandalwood from this shore of the ocean as a pyre, he made offerings to the Buddha’s body, and cremated it.

After the fire died out, he gathered the relics, made eighty-four thousand precious urns, and erected eighty-four thousand stūpas, three worlds in height, with adorned spires, hanging banners, canopies, and jewel bells.

Then Sarvasattvapriyadarśana Bodhisattva thought to himself again: ‘Although I have made these offerings, my mind is not yet satisfied; I should now make further offerings to the relics.’

He said to the Bodhisattva great disciples, devas, Nagas, yakṣas, and the entire assembly: ‘You should all be single-mindedly mindful; I now make offerings to the relics of Candrasūryavimalaprabhāsaśrī Buddha.’

Having spoken these words, before the eighty-four thousand stūpas, he burned his arm adorned with a hundred merits for seventy-two thousand years as an offering, causing countless multitudes seeking to be Śrāvakas and immeasurable incalculable people to generate the mind of anuttarasamyaksambodhi, all enabling them to dwell in the samādhi of manifesting all physical bodies.

At that time, the Bodhisattvas, devas, humans, and asuras, seeing him without an arm, grieved, sorrowed, and lamented, saying: ‘This Sarvasattvapriyadarśana Bodhisattva is our teacher who taught and transformed us; yet now he has burned his arm, his body incomplete!’

Then Sarvasattvapriyadarśana Bodhisattva, amidst the great assembly, made this vow: ‘Having cast off both arms, I will certainly attain the golden body of a Buddha; if this is genuine and not false, may both my arms be restored as before!’

Having made this vow, they were naturally restored as before, brought about by the deep purity of this Bodhisattva’s merit and wisdom. At that moment, the great three-thousand-fold worlds shook in six ways, heaven rained down jewel flowers, and all humans and devas attained what they had never had before.”

The Buddha addressed Nakṣatrarājasamkusumitābhijña Bodhisattva: “What do you think: could Sarvasattvapriyadarśana Bodhisattva have been someone else? It was Bhaiṣajyarāja Bodhisattva himself. The instances of his abandoning his body in giving are immeasurable hundreds of thousands of millions of inconceivable numbers like this.

Nakṣatrarājasamkusumitābhijña! If someone generates the resolve wishing to attain anuttarasamyaksambodhi, and is able to burn a finger or even a single toe as an offering to a Buddha stūpa, it surpasses offering countries, cities, wives, children, and the mountains, forests, rivers, ponds, and treasures of the great three-thousand-fold worlds.

If someone fills the great three-thousand-fold worlds with the seven treasures to make offerings to the Buddha, great Bodhisattvas, Pratyekabuddhas, and arhats, the merit attained by that person does not equal receiving and upholding this Lotus Sūtra, down to a single four-line gāthā; its merit is foremost!

Nakṣatrarājasamkusumitābhijña! Just as among all streams, rivers, and waters, the ocean is foremost; this Lotus Sūtra is also like this: among scriptures expounded by the Tathāgatas, it is the deepest and greatest.

Just as among earthen mountains, black mountains, the Small Iron Ring Mountains, the Great Iron Ring Mountains, and the ten jewel mountains, Mount Sumeru is foremost; this Lotus Sūtra is also like this: among all scriptures, it stands supreme.

Just as among the stars, the Moon Deva is foremost; this Lotus Sūtra is also like this: among thousands of billions of scriptural dharmas, it illuminates most brightly.

Just as the Sun Deva can dispel all darkness; this scripture is also like this: able to shatter all darkness of unwholesome things.

Just as among petty kings, the wheel-turning sage king is foremost; this scripture is also like this: among all scriptures, it is most revered.

Just as Śakra is king among the Trāyastriṃśa devas; this scripture is also like this: the King among scriptures.

Just as the Great Brahmā King is the father of all wandering beings; this scripture is also like this: the father of all worthy sages, practitioners in training and beyond training, and those who generate the Bodhisattva resolve.

Just as among all common mortals, the srotāpanna, sakṛdāgāmin, anāgāmin, arhat, and pratyekabuddha are foremost; this scripture is also like this: among all scriptural dharmas expounded by Tathāgatas, Bodhisattvas, or Śrāvakas, it is foremost. Those who can receive and uphold this scripture are also like this: among all wandering beings, they are foremost.

Among all Śrāvakas and Pratyekabuddhas, the Bodhisattva is foremost; this scripture is also like this: among all scriptural dharmas, it is foremost.

Just as the Buddha is the King of all dharmas; this scripture is also like this: the King of all scriptures.

Nakṣatrarājasamkusumitābhijña! This scripture can rescue all wandering beings; this scripture can cause all wandering beings to be free of suffering and afflictions; this scripture can greatly benefit all wandering beings, fulfilling their vows.

Like a cool, clear pond, able to satisfy all who are thirsty; like the cold finding fire, like the naked finding clothing, like merchants finding a caravan leader, like a child finding a mother, like a ferry finding a boat, like the sick finding a physician, like darkness finding a lamp, like the poor finding treasure, like subjects finding a king, like traders finding the ocean, like a torch dispelling darkness: this Lotus Sūtra is also like this, able to cause wandering beings to abandon all suffering and illness, able to untie all the bonds of birth and death.

If someone hears this Lotus Sūtra, whether copying it oneself or causing others to copy it, the merit attained, calculating it with the Buddha’s wisdom, cannot reach its limit. If copying this scriptural scroll, making offerings of flowers, incense, necklaces, burning incense, powdered incense, paste incense, banners, canopies, clothing, and various lamps—ghee lamps, oil lamps, scented oil lamps, campaka oil lamps, sumanā oil lamps, pāṭala oil lamps, vārṣikā oil lamps, and navamallikā oil lamps—the merit attained is also immeasurable.

Nakṣatrarājasamkusumitābhijña! If someone hears this Former activities of Bhaiṣajyarāja Bodhisattva Chapter, they also attain immeasurable, boundless merit. If a woman hears this Former activities of Bhaiṣajyarāja Bodhisattva Chapter and can receive and uphold it, when this female body ends, she will never receive a female body again.

If after the Tathāgata’s extinction, during the final five hundred years, a woman hears this scripture and practices as taught: dying here, she will immediately go to the Land of Joyful Ease, where Amitābha Buddha dwells surrounded by the assembly of great Bodhisattvas, born upon a jewel seat within a lotus flower, no longer afflicted by greed and lust, nor afflicted by anger and delusion, nor afflicted by pride, envy, and defilements; attaining the Bodhisattva’s spiritual superknowledges and the forbearance of non-arising dharmas.

Having attained this forbearance, her eye faculty is pure; with this pure eye faculty, she beholds Buddhas, Tathāgatas equal to the sands of seven hundred and two thousand billion inconceivable numbers of Ganges rivers.

At that time, the Buddhas praise her from afar: ‘Excellent, excellent! Good son! That within the Dharma of Śākyamuni Buddha you were able to receive, uphold, read, recite, and contemplate this scripture, and expound it for others, the merit you attain is immeasurable and boundless; fire cannot burn it, water cannot sweep it away; your virtues, a thousand Buddhas speaking together could not exhaust. You have now shattered the thieves of Mara, destroyed the army of birth and death, and crushed all other enemies. Good son! Hundreds of thousands of Buddhas guard you together through spiritual powers; in all the world, among devas and humans, none is equal to you, except the Tathāgata; among Śrāvakas, Pratyekabuddhas, down to Bodhisattvas, none possess wisdom and dhyāna meditation equal to yours!’

Nakṣatrarājasamkusumitābhijña! This Bodhisattva accomplishes such power of virtue and wisdom. If someone hears this Former activities of Bhaiṣajyarāja Bodhisattva Chapter, and can rejoice in accord and praise its goodness, in this present life the fragrance of a blue lotus flower will constantly issue from their mouth, and the fragrance of ox-head sandalwood will constantly issue from the pores of their body; the merits attained are as spoken above.

Therefore, Nakṣatrarājasamkusumitābhijña! I entrust this Former activities of Bhaiṣajyarāja Bodhisattva Chapter to you. After my extinction, in the final five hundred years, widely proclaim and propagate it in Jambudvīpa, do not allow it to be cut off, lest evil Maras, the subjects of Mara, devas, Nagas, yakṣas, and kumbhāṇḍas gain an opening!

Nakṣatrarājasamkusumitābhijña! You should guard this scripture through spiritual powers. For what reason? This scripture is good medicine for the illnesses of people in Jambudvīpa. If someone is ill and hears this scripture, their illness will be eliminated, free of old age and death.

Nakṣatrarājasamkusumitābhijña! If you see someone who upholds this scripture, you should take a blue lotus flower filled with powdered incense and scatter it over them as an offering. Having scattered it, have this thought: ‘This person before long will certainly take grass and sit at the place of awakening, shattering the armies of Mara, blowing the Dharma conch, striking the great Dharma drum, and liberating all wandering beings from the ocean of old age, sickness, and death.’

Therefore, those who seek the Buddha path, seeing those who uphold this scripture, should give rise to such a reverent mind as this.”

When this Former activities of Bhaiṣajyarāja Bodhisattva Chapter was expounded, eighty-four thousand Bodhisattvas attained the dhāraṇī of understanding the languages of all wandering beings.

Prabhūtaratna Tathāgata, from within the jewel stūpa, praised Nakṣatrarājasamkusumitābhijña Bodhisattva: “Excellent, excellent, Nakṣatrarājasamkusumitābhijña! You have accomplished inconceivable merits, that you are able to question Śākyamuni Buddha on such matters as these, benefiting immeasurable wandering beings!”

← Capítulo anteriorPróximo capítulo →

Capítulo XXIV

O bodhisattva Gadgadasvara

Este capítulo ainda está em tradução para o português; é exibido provisoriamente em inglês.

At that time, Śākyamuni Buddha radiated a light from the flesh-topknot of the mark of a great person, and radiated a light from the white hair between his eyebrows, universally illuminating the Buddha worlds of one million eighty thousand billion inconceivable numbers of Ganges sands in the eastern direction.

Past these numbers, there was a world named Virocanaraśmipratimaṇḍita. In that land was a Buddha named Jaladharagarjitasvaranakṣatrarājasaṃkusumitābhijña Tathāgata, Arhat, Samyaksambuddha, Vidyācaraṇasampanna, Sugata, Lokavid, Anuttara, Puruṣadamyasārathi, Śāstā Devamanuṣyāṇām, Buddha, Bhagavān, reverently surrounded by an immeasurable, boundless assembly of Bodhisattvas for whom he expounded the Dharma. The white hair radiant light of Śākyamuni Buddha universally illuminated that land.

At that time, in the land Virocanaraśmipratimaṇḍita, there was a Bodhisattva named Gadgadasvara, who had long planted roots of virtue, making offerings to and attending upon immeasurable hundreds of thousands of millions of Buddhas, fully accomplishing profound wisdom. He had attained the Wonderful Banner Mark samādhi, the Lotus of Dharma samādhi, the Pure Virtue samādhi, the Constellation King’s Sport samādhi, the Objectless samādhi, the Wisdom Seal samādhi, the Understanding All Wandering Beings’ Languages samādhi, the Gathering All Virtues samādhi, the Purity samādhi, the Sport of Spiritual Superknowledges samādhi, the Torch of Wisdom samādhi, the King of Adornment samādhi, the Pure Light samādhi, the Pure Treasury samādhi, the Distinct samādhi, and the Sun-Rotation samādhi, attaining hundreds of thousands of millions of Ganges sands of great samādhis such as these.

When the light of Śākyamuni Buddha illuminated his body, he immediately addressed Jaladharagarjitasvaranakṣatrarājasaṃkusumitābhijña Buddha: “World-Honored One! I should go to the Sahā world to bow down, draw near, and make offerings to Śākyamuni Buddha, and to see Mañjuśrī, Prince of the Dharma Bodhisattva, Bhaiṣajyarāja Bodhisattva, Pradānaśūra Bodhisattva, Nakṣatrarājasamkusumitābhijña Bodhisattva, Viśiṣṭacāritra Bodhisattva, Śubhavyūharāja Bodhisattva, and Bhaiṣajyasamudgata Bodhisattva.”

Then Jaladharagarjitasvaranakṣatrarājasaṃkusumitābhijña Buddha addressed Gadgadasvara Bodhisattva: “Do not look down upon that land and give rise to thoughts of its inferiority. Good son! That Sahā world is uneven with heights and depths; its earth and stone mountains are filled with filth and evil; the Buddha’s body is small and low, and the Bodhisattva assemblies are also small in stature. Yet your body is forty-two thousand yojanas in height, and my body is six million eight hundred thousand yojanas; your form is foremost in beauty, with hundreds of thousands of merits, its radiance extraordinary. Therefore, when you go, do not look down upon that land, giving rise to thoughts of inferiority toward the Buddha, Bodhisattvas, or the realm.”

Gadgadasvara Bodhisattva addressed that Buddha: “World-Honored One! That I now travel to the Sahā world is entirely through the Tathāgata’s power, the Tathāgata’s sporting in spiritual powers, and the Tathāgata’s adornments of merit and wisdom.”

Thereupon, Gadgadasvara Bodhisattva, without rising from his seat and without moving his body, entered samādhi. Through the power of samādhi, on Mount Gṛdhrakūṭa, not far from the Dharma seat, there appeared eighty-four thousand jewel lotuses, with stalks of Jāmbūnada gold, leaves of white silver, stamens of diamond, and calyxes of kimśuka jewels.

At that time, Mañjuśrī, Prince of the Dharma, seeing these lotus flowers, addressed the Buddha: “World-Honored One! For what causal condition has this auspicious sign first appeared? There are several tens of thousands of lotus flowers, with stalks of Jāmbūnada gold, leaves of white silver, stamens of diamond, and calyxes of kimśuka jewels.”

Then Śākyamuni Buddha addressed Mañjuśrī: “This is Gadgadasvara Bodhisattva Mahāsattva, who wishes to come from the land of Jaladharagarjitasvaranakṣatrarājasaṃkusumitābhijña Buddha, surrounded by eighty-four thousand Bodhisattvas, arriving in this Sahā world to make offerings, draw near, and bow to me, and also wishing to make offerings to and hear the Lotus Sūtra.”

Mañjuśrī addressed the Buddha: “World-Honored One! What roots of virtue did this Bodhisattva plant, and what merits did he cultivate, to be able to possess such great spiritual powers? What samādhi does he practice? Pray speak the names of these samādhis for us; we too wish diligently to cultivate them; practicing this samādhi, we will be able to behold the size of this Bodhisattva’s physical appearance, and his deportment in advancing and halting. We only pray that the World-Honored One will use spiritual power to cause that Bodhisattva to arrive so that we may see him.”

Then Śākyamuni Buddha addressed Mañjuśrī: “Prabhūtaratna Tathāgata, who has long passed into extinction, will manifest his appearance for you all.”

Then Prabhūtaratna Buddha addressed that Bodhisattva: “Good son! Come; Mañjuśrī, Prince of the Dharma, wishes to see your body.”

Thereupon, Gadgadasvara Bodhisattva vanished from that land and departed together with eighty-four thousand Bodhisattvas. The lands they passed through shook in six ways, all raining seven-jeweled lotus flowers; hundreds of thousands of heavenly instruments sounded of themselves without being struck.

This Bodhisattva’s eyes were like broad blue lotus petals; even if combining hundreds of thousands of moons, his facial countenance was more upright and handsome than this; his body was of pure golden color, adorned with immeasurable hundreds of thousands of merits, ablaze with majesty and virtue, radiant with light, endowed with all marks, firm as the body of Nārāyaṇa.

Entering a seven-jeweled tower, he ascended into open space to the height of seven tāla trees, reverently surrounded by the Bodhisattva assembly, arriving at Mount Gṛdhrakūṭa in this Sahā world. Having arrived, he stepped down from the seven-jeweled tower, took necklaces worth hundreds of thousands, proceeded to where Śākyamuni Buddha was, bowed with his head touching the Buddha’s feet, presented the necklaces, and addressed the Buddha:

“World-Honored One! Jaladharagarjitasvaranakṣatrarājasaṃkusumitābhijña Buddha inquires after the World-Honored One: Are you free of sickness and affliction, light and energetic in daily life, practicing in peace and joy? Are the four great elements in harmony? Are worldly activities endurable? Are wandering beings easy to liberate? Are they free from excessive greed, anger, delusion, envy, stinginess, and arrogance? Are they free from unfilial conduct toward parents, irreverence toward śramaṇas, false views, unwholesome minds, and unbridled five passions?

World-Honored One! Are wandering beings able to subdue the enemies of Mara? Does Prabhūtaratna Tathāgata, long passed into extinction within the seven-jeweled stūpa, come to hear the Dharma? He also inquires after Prabhūtaratna Tathāgata: Is he at peace, free of affliction, able to endure a long stay? World-Honored One! I now wish to behold the body of Prabhūtaratna Buddha; we only pray that the World-Honored One will show it to me, causing me to see him.”

Then Śākyamuni Buddha said to Prabhūtaratna Buddha: “This Gadgadasvara Bodhisattva wishes to meet you.”

Then Prabhūtaratna Buddha addressed Gadgadasvara: “Excellent, excellent! That you, for the sake of making offerings to Śākyamuni Buddha, hearing the Lotus Sūtra, and seeing Mañjuśrī and the others, have arrived here.”

At that time, Padmaśrī Bodhisattva addressed the Buddha: “World-Honored One! What roots of virtue did this Gadgadasvara Bodhisattva plant, and what merits did he cultivate, to possess such spiritual powers?”

The Buddha addressed Padmaśrī Bodhisattva: “In the past, there was a Buddha named Meghadundubhisvararāja Tathāgata, Arhat, Samyaksambuddha. His land was named Sarvalokasaṃdarśana, and his kalpa was named Priyadarśana. For twelve thousand years, Gadgadasvara Bodhisattva, with a hundred thousand kinds of music, made offerings to Meghadundubhisvararāja Buddha, and presented eighty-four thousand alms bowls of the seven treasures.

Through the causal conditions of this karmic retribution, he was born into the land of Jaladharagarjitasvaranakṣatrarājasaṃkusumitābhijña Buddha, possessing such spiritual powers.

Padmaśrī! What do you think: could Gadgadasvara Bodhisattva who at that time made music offerings and presented precious vessels to Meghadundubhisvararāja Buddha have been someone else? It was this Gadgadasvara Bodhisattva Mahāsattva himself.

Padmaśrī! This Gadgadasvara Bodhisattva had already made offerings to and attended upon immeasurable Buddhas, long planting roots of virtue, and had encountered hundreds of thousands of millions of inconceivable numbers of Buddhas equal to the sands of the Ganges.

Padmaśrī! You see only the body of Gadgadasvara Bodhisattva present here; yet this Bodhisattva manifests various bodies, in various places expounding this scripture for wandering beings:

Sometimes manifesting as a Brahmā King, sometimes manifesting as Śakra, sometimes manifesting as Īśvara, sometimes manifesting as Maheśvara, sometimes manifesting as a heavenly great general, sometimes manifesting as Vaiśravaṇa Heavenly King, sometimes manifesting as a wheel-turning sage king, sometimes manifesting as a petty king, sometimes manifesting as an elder, sometimes manifesting as a householder, sometimes manifesting as an official, sometimes manifesting as a brāhmaṇa, sometimes manifesting as a bhikṣu, bhikṣuṇī, upāsaka, or upāsikā;

Sometimes manifesting as the wife of an elder, householder, official, or brāhmaṇa, sometimes manifesting as a boy or girl; sometimes manifesting as a deva, nāga, yakṣa, gandharva, asura, garuḍa, kiṃnara, mahoraga, human, or non-human body, expounding this scripture.

All those in hells, hungry ghosts, animals, and difficult realms, he is able to rescue; down to transforming into a woman’s body in royal inner palaces to expound this scripture.

Padmaśrī! This Gadgadasvara Bodhisattva is one who can rescue and protect wandering beings of the Sahā world. This Gadgadasvara Bodhisattva, through such various manifesting transformations of body, in this Sahā world expounds this scripture for wandering beings, without his spiritual superknowledges, transformations, or wisdom suffering any diminishment.

This Bodhisattva, with various wisdoms, clearly illuminates the Sahā world, causing all wandering beings each to attain what they should know; and in worlds of the ten directions equal to the sands of the Ganges, it is also like this.

If they should be liberated through the form of a Śrāvaka, he manifests the form of a Śrāvaka to expound the Dharma; if they should be liberated through the form of a Pratyekabuddha, he manifests the form of a Pratyekabuddha to expound the Dharma; if they should be liberated through the form of a Bodhisattva, he manifests the form of a Bodhisattva to expound the Dharma; if they should be liberated through the form of a Buddha, he immediately manifests the form of a Buddha to expound the Dharma. Various forms like these he manifests according to what is appropriate to liberate them, down to manifesting extinction for those who should be liberated through extinction.

Padmaśrī! Gadgadasvara Bodhisattva Mahāsattva has accomplished such power of great spiritual superknowledges and wisdom.”

Then Padmaśrī Bodhisattva addressed the Buddha: “World-Honored One! This Gadgadasvara Bodhisattva has deeply planted roots of virtue. World-Honored One! In what samādhi does this Bodhisattva dwell, that he is able to manifest transformations in this manner to liberate wandering beings?”

The Buddha addressed Padmaśrī Bodhisattva: “Good son! That samādhi is named Manifesting All Physical Bodies. Gadgadasvara Bodhisattva, dwelling within this samādhi, is able in this manner to benefit immeasurable wandering beings.”

When this Bodhisattva Gadgadasvara Chapter was expounded, the eighty-four thousand persons who had accompanied Gadgadasvara Bodhisattva all attained the samādhi of Manifesting All Physical Bodies; immeasurable Bodhisattvas of this Sahā world also attained this samādhi and dhāraṇīs.

Then Gadgadasvara Bodhisattva Mahāsattva, having made offerings to Śākyamuni Buddha and the stūpa of Prabhūtaratna Buddha, returned to his native land. The lands he passed through shook in six ways, raining jewel lotuses, and producing hundreds of thousands of millions of varieties of music.

Having arrived in his native land, surrounded by eighty-four thousand Bodhisattvas, he proceeded to where Jaladharagarjitasvaranakṣatrarājasaṃkusumitābhijña Buddha was, and addressed the Buddha: “World-Honored One! I arrived in the Sahā world and benefited wandering beings; I beheld Śākyamuni Buddha, and beheld the stūpa of Prabhūtaratna Buddha, bowing and making offerings. I also saw Mañjuśrī, Prince of the Dharma Bodhisattva, as well as Bhaiṣajyarāja Bodhisattva, Viryabalavegadhārin Bodhisattva, and Pradānaśūra Bodhisattva, and caused these eighty-four thousand Bodhisattvas to attain the samādhi of Manifesting All Physical Bodies.”

When this chapter on the Coming and Going of Bodhisattva Gadgadasvara was expounded, forty-two thousand sons of devas attained the forbearance of non-arising dharmas, and Padmaśrī Bodhisattva attained the Lotus of Dharma samādhi.

← Capítulo anteriorPróximo capítulo →

Capítulo XXV

A porta universal do bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara

Este capítulo ainda está em tradução para o português; é exibido provisoriamente em inglês.

At that time, Akṣayamati Bodhisattva arose from his seat, bared his right shoulder, joined his palms toward the Buddha, and spoke these words: “World-Honored One! By what causal condition is Bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara named Avalokiteśvara?”

The Buddha addressed Akṣayamati Bodhisattva: “Good son! If there are immeasurable hundreds of thousands of millions of wandering beings undergoing various sufferings and afflictions, who, hearing of this Bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara, single-mindedly recite his name, Bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara will immediately perceive their sounds, and they will all attain liberation.

If someone upholds the name of this Bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara, even if entering a great fire, the fire cannot burn them, through the majestic spiritual power of this Bodhisattva.

If swept away by great waters, reciting his name, they will immediately reach a shallow place.

If there are hundreds of thousands of millions of wandering beings who, seeking gold, silver, lapis lazuli, mother-of-pearl, agate, coral, amber, pearls, and other treasures, enter the great ocean; and suppose a black wind blows their ships, drifting into the land of rākṣasa ghosts: if among them there is even a single person who recites the name of Bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara, all those people will attain liberation from the perils of rākṣasas.

By this causal condition, he is named Avalokiteśvara.

If someone about to be harmed recites the name of Bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara, the knives and sticks held by the assailant will instantly break into pieces, and they will attain liberation.

If a great three-thousand-fold worlds were filled with yakṣas and rākṣasas wishing to torment people, hearing them recite the name of Bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara, those evil ghosts could not even look upon them with an evil eye, how much less inflict harm!

Even if someone, whether guilty or innocent, has their body bound in fetters, stocks, cangues, and chains, reciting the name of Bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara, all will break apart, and they will immediately attain liberation.

If a great three-thousand-fold worlds were filled with dangerous bandits, and a merchant leader leading various traders carrying valuable treasures travels along a perilous road; among them, one person speaks out: ‘Good children! Do not harbor fear! You should all single-mindedly recite the name of Bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara. This Bodhisattva can bestow fearlessness upon wandering beings. If you recite his name, from these dangerous bandits you will attain liberation!’ All the traders, hearing this, simultaneously raise their voices: ‘Homage to Bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara!’ Because of reciting his name, they immediately attain liberation.

Akṣayamati! The majestic spiritual power of Bodhisattva Mahāsattva Avalokiteśvara is towering like this!

If wandering beings have excessive desire, constantly remembering and revering Bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara, they will abandon desire.

If having excessive anger, constantly remembering and revering Bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara, they will abandon anger.

If having excessive ignorance, constantly remembering and revering Bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara, they will abandon ignorance.

Akṣayamati! Bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara possesses such great majestic spiritual powers, extensively bringing benefit. Therefore, wandering beings should constantly be mindful of him in their hearts.

If a woman wishes to seek a son, bowing down and making offerings to Bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara, she will give birth to a son of merit, virtue, and wisdom; if she wishes to seek a daughter, she will give birth to an upright, handsome daughter, who has planted roots of virtue in past lives, loved and revered by all.

Akṣayamati! Bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara possesses such power. If wandering beings revere and bow down to Bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara, their blessings will not be in vain. Therefore, wandering beings should all receive and uphold the name of Bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara.

Akṣayamati! If someone were to receive and uphold the names of Bodhisattvas equal to the sands of sixty-two billion Ganges rivers, and for the rest of their life make offerings of food, drink, clothing, bedding, and medicine: what do you think: is the merit of this good man or good woman plentiful or not?”

Akṣayamati said: “Extremely plentiful, World-Honored One!”

The Buddha said: “If someone receives and upholds the name of Bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara, and even for an instant bows down and makes offerings: the blessings of these two persons are equal without difference, and throughout hundreds of thousands of millions of kalpas cannot be exhausted.

Akṣayamati! Receiving and upholding the name of Bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara attains such immeasurable, boundless benefits of merit.”

Akṣayamati Bodhisattva addressed the Buddha: “World-Honored One! How does Bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara travel in this Sahā world? How does he expound the Dharma for wandering beings? How does he exercise the power of expedient means?”

The Buddha addressed Akṣayamati Bodhisattva: “Good son! If wandering beings in a land should be liberated through the body of a Buddha, Bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara immediately manifests the body of a Buddha to expound the Dharma;

If they should be liberated through the body of a Pratyekabuddha, he immediately manifests the body of a Pratyekabuddha to expound the Dharma;

If they should be liberated through the body of a Śrāvaka, he immediately manifests the body of a Śrāvaka to expound the Dharma;

If they should be liberated through the body of a Brahmā King, he immediately manifests the body of a Brahmā King to expound the Dharma;

If they should be liberated through the body of Śakra, he immediately manifests the body of Śakra to expound the Dharma;

If they should be liberated through the body of Īśvara, he immediately manifests the body of Īśvara to expound the Dharma;

If they should be liberated through the body of Maheśvara, he immediately manifests the body of Maheśvara to expound the Dharma;

If they should be liberated through the body of a heavenly great general, he immediately manifests the body of a heavenly great general to expound the Dharma;

If they should be liberated through the body of Vaiśravaṇa, he immediately manifests the body of Vaiśravaṇa to expound the Dharma;

If they should be liberated through the body of a petty king, he immediately manifests the body of a petty king to expound the Dharma;

If they should be liberated through the body of an elder, he immediately manifests the body of an elder to expound the Dharma;

If they should be liberated through the body of a householder, he immediately manifests the body of a householder to expound the Dharma;

If they should be liberated through the body of an official, he immediately manifests the body of an official to expound the Dharma;

If they should be liberated through the body of a brāhmaṇa, he immediately manifests the body of a brāhmaṇa to expound the Dharma;

If they should be liberated through the body of a bhikṣu, bhikṣuṇī, upāsaka, or upāsikā, he immediately manifests the body of a bhikṣu, bhikṣuṇī, upāsaka, or upāsikā to expound the Dharma;

If they should be liberated through the body of the wife of an elder, householder, official, or brāhmaṇa, he immediately manifests the body of a woman to expound the Dharma;

If they should be liberated through the body of a boy or girl, he immediately manifests the body of a boy or girl to expound the Dharma;

If they should be liberated through the bodies of devas, nāgas, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kiṃnaras, mahoragas, humans, or non-humans, he immediately manifests them all to expound the Dharma;

If they should be liberated through the body of Vajrapāṇi, he immediately manifests the body of Vajrapāṇi to expound the Dharma.

Akṣayamati! This Bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara has accomplished such merits, through various forms traveling in lands to liberate wandering beings. Therefore, you all should single-mindedly make offerings to Bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara. This Bodhisattva Mahāsattva Avalokiteśvara can bestow fearlessness amidst terrors and pressing perils; therefore, in this Sahā world, all call him the Bestower of Fearlessness.”

Akṣayamati Bodhisattva addressed the Buddha: “World-Honored One! I should now make an offering to Bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara.”

Immediately he unfastened from his neck a necklace of precious pearls worth hundreds of thousands of pieces of gold, gave it to him, and said: “Virtuous one! Accept this Dharma offering of precious pearl necklaces.”

At that time, Bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara was unwilling to accept it. Akṣayamati addressed Bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara again: “Virtuous one! Out of compassion for us, accept this necklace.”

Then the Buddha addressed Bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara: “Out of compassion for this Akṣayamati Bodhisattva, the fourfold assembly, devas, Nagas, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kiṃnaras, mahoragas, humans, and non-humans, accept this necklace.”

Immediately Bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara, out of compassion for the fourfold assembly, devas, Nagas, humans, and non-humans, accepted the necklace, divided it into two parts, presenting one part to Śākyamuni Buddha, and presenting one part to the stūpa of Prabhūtaratna Buddha.

“Akṣayamati! Bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara possesses such sovereign spiritual powers, traveling in the Sahā world.”

Thereupon, Akṣayamati Bodhisattva inquired in gāthās:

Endowed with wondrous marks, World-Honored One,

I now question you again:

By what causal condition,

Is this Buddha-child named Avalokiteśvara?

The Honored One endowed with wondrous marks,

Answered Akṣayamati in gāthās:

Listen to the conduct of Avalokiteśvara,

Skillfully responding in all directions;

His vast vows are deep as the ocean,

Inconceivable through kalpas;

Attending upon thousands of billions of Buddhas,

Generating great pure vows.

I will briefly speak for you:

Hearing his name and beholding his body,

Mindfulness in heart is not in vain,

Able to extinguish all sufferings of existence.

Even if someone harboring harmful intent,

Pushes one into a great fire pit,

Mindful of that power of Avalokiteśvara,

The fire pit transforms into a pool of water.

Or if drifting upon the great ocean,

Beset by perils of Nagas, fish, and ghosts,

Mindful of that power of Avalokiteśvara,

Waves cannot drown them.

Or if on the peak of Mount Sumeru,

One is pushed down by someone,

Mindful of that power of Avalokiteśvara,

Like the sun one abides in open space.

Or if pursued by evil people,

Falling from a Diamond Mountain,

Mindful of that power of Avalokiteśvara,

Not a single hair can be harmed.

Or if surrounded by hostile bandits,

Each holding knives to inflict harm,

Mindful of that power of Avalokiteśvara,

They immediately give rise to compassionate minds.

Or if encountering suffering under royal execution,

Approaching death at the place of punishment,

Mindful of that power of Avalokiteśvara,

The knives instantly break into pieces.

Or if imprisoned in stocks and chains,

Hands and feet bound in fetters,

Mindful of that power of Avalokiteśvara,

They are broken open and liberation is attained.

Curses and various poisonous herbs,

Intended to harm one’s body,

Mindful of that power of Avalokiteśvara,

They return upon the perpetrator.

Or if encountering evil rākṣasas,

Poisonous Nagas, and ghosts,

Mindful of that power of Avalokiteśvara,

At that moment none dare inflict harm.

If surrounded by evil beasts,

With terrifying sharp fangs and claws,

Mindful of that power of Avalokiteśvara,

They flee swiftly in boundless directions.

Vipers, adders, and scorpions,

Breathing poisonous vapor like blazing fires,

Mindful of that power of Avalokiteśvara,

Accordant with the sound, they turn away.

Clouds thunder and lightning flashes,

Hail falls and great rain pours,

Mindful of that power of Avalokiteśvara,

In due time they disperse.

When wandering beings are beset by peril,

Immeasurable sufferings pressing upon their bodies,

Avalokiteśvara’s wondrous power of wisdom,

Can rescue the suffering of the world.

Endowed with spiritual powers,

Extensively cultivating wisdom and expedient means,

In lands of the ten directions,

There is no realm where he does not manifest.

Various evil destinies,

Hells, ghosts, and animals,

The suffering of birth, old age, sickness, and death,

Gradually are all caused to cease.

Genuine contemplation, pure contemplation,

Vast wisdom contemplation,

Compassionate contemplation, and loving contemplation,

Constantly prayed for, constantly looked up to!

Pure light without defilement,

The sun of wisdom dispels all darkness,

Able to subdue calamities of wind and fire,

Universally illuminating the world.

The body of compassion is thunderous like precepts,

The mind of love is a wondrous great cloud,

Raining down the amṛta Dharma rain,

Extinguishing the flames of afflictions.

In lawsuits before officials,

And amidst terrifying battle arrays,

Mindful of that power of Avalokiteśvara,

All hostilities disperse and retreat.

Wondrous sound, Avalokiteśvara,

Brahma-sound, ocean tide sound,

Surpassing worldly sounds;

Therefore one must constantly be mindful!

Thought after thought, do not give rise to doubt;

Avalokiteśvara, the pure sage,

In suffering, affliction, and the peril of death,

Can serve as a refuge.

Endowed with all virtues,

Beholding wandering beings with eyes of compassion,

An ocean of accumulated merit without measure;

Therefore one should bow down in homage!

At that time, Dharaṇīṃdhara Bodhisattva arose from his seat, went forward, and addressed the Buddha: “World-Honored One! If there are wandering beings who hear this chapter of Bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara, his sovereign deeds and spiritual powers of universal gate manifestation, you should know that this person’s merit is not small.”

When the Buddha expounded this Universal Gate Chapter, eighty-four thousand wandering beings in the assembly all generated the mind of anuttarasamyaksambodhi, which is beyond compare.

Capítulo XXVI

Dhāraṇī

Este capítulo ainda está em tradução para o português; é exibido provisoriamente em inglês.

At that time, Bhaiṣajyarāja Bodhisattva arose from his seat, bared his right shoulder, joined his palms toward the Buddha, and addressed the Buddha: “World-Honored One! If a good man or good woman is able to receive and uphold the Lotus Sūtra—whether reading, reciting fluently, or copying the scriptural scrolls—how much merit do they attain?”

The Buddha addressed Bhaiṣajyarāja: “If a good man or good woman makes offerings to Buddhas equal to eight hundred billion inconceivable numbers of Ganges sands: what do you think: is the merit attained plentiful or not?”

“Extremely plentiful, World-Honored One.”

The Buddha said: “If a good man or good woman, regarding this scripture, can receive and uphold even a single four-line gāthā, reading, reciting, understanding its meaning, and practicing as taught, their merit is exceedingly plentiful.”

At that time, Bhaiṣajyarāja Bodhisattva addressed the Buddha: “World-Honored One! I will now bestow a dhāraṇī mantra upon expounders of the Dharma to protect them.”

Immediately he spoke the mantra:

ANYE MANYE MANE MAMANE CIRE CARITE ŚAME ŚAMITĀ VIŚĀNTE MUKTE MUKTATAME ŚAME AVIṢAME SAMASAME JAYE KṢAYE KṢĪṆE ŚĀNTE ŚAMITE DHĀRAṆĪ ĀLOKABHĀSE PRATYAVEKṢAṆI NIVIṢṬE ABHYANTARANIVIṢṬE ATYANTAPĀRIŚUDDHI UTKULE MUKULE ARALE PARABALE ŚUKLĀVAKṢE ASAMASAME BUDDHAVILOKITE DHARMAPARĪKṢITE SAṄGHANIRGHOṢAṆI NIRGHOṢA NIRGHOṢI MANTRE MANTRĀKṢAYATE RUTE RUTAKAUŚALYE AKṢAYE AKṢAYATĀYĀṂ AVALO AMĀNYANATĀYA SVĀHĀ

“World-Honored One! This dhāraṇī divine mantra was spoken by Buddhas equal to sixty-two billion Ganges sands. If someone invades or defames this Dharma teacher, they have invaded and defamed these Buddhas.”

Then Śākyamuni Buddha praised Bhaiṣajyarāja Bodhisattva: “Excellent, excellent, Bhaiṣajyarāja! That out of compassion to protect this Dharma teacher you speak this dhāraṇī, bringing abundant benefit to wandering beings.”

At that time, Pradānaśūra Bodhisattva addressed the Buddha: “World-Honored One! I too, to protect those who read, recite, receive, and uphold the Lotus Sūtra, will speak a dhāraṇī. If this Dharma teacher attains this dhāraṇī, whether yakṣas, rākṣasas, pūtanas, kṛtyas, kumbhāṇḍas, or hungry ghosts seek their shortcomings, none will gain an opening.”

Immediately before the Buddha he spoke the mantra:

JVALE MAHĀJVALE UKKE TUKKE MUKKE AḌE AḌĀVATI NṚTYE NṚTYĀVATI IṬṬINI VIṬṬINI CIṬṬINI NṚTYĀNI NṚTYĀVATI SVĀHĀ

“World-Honored One! This dhāraṇī divine mantra was spoken by Buddhas equal to the sands of the Ganges, who all rejoice in accord. If someone invades or defames this Dharma teacher, they have invaded and defamed these Buddhas.”

At that time, Vaiśravaṇa Heavenly King, protector of the world, addressed the Buddha: “World-Honored One! I too, out of compassion for wandering beings and to protect this Dharma teacher, will speak this dhāraṇī.”

Immediately he spoke the mantra:

AṬṬE TAṬṬE NAṬṬE VANAṬṬE ANAḌE NĀḌI KUNAḌI SVĀHĀ

“World-Honored One! With this divine mantra I protect the Dharma teacher; I myself will also protect those who uphold this scripture, causing them within a hundred yojanas to be free of all misfortunes and decline.”

At that time, Dhṛtarāṣṭra Heavenly King was present in this assembly, reverently surrounded by tens of billions of inconceivable numbers of gandharvas; proceeding before the Buddha, he joined his palms and addressed the Buddha: “World-Honored One! I too, with a dhāraṇī divine mantra, will protect those who uphold the Lotus Sūtra.”

Immediately he spoke the mantra:

AGAṆE GAṆE GAURĪ GANDHĀRI CAṆḌĀLI MĀTAṄGĪ PUKKASĪ SAMAŚI SVASTI SVĀHĀ

“World-Honored One! This dhāraṇī divine mantra was spoken by forty-two billion Buddhas. If someone invades or defames this Dharma teacher, they have invaded and defamed these Buddhas.”

At that time, there were rākṣasīs: the first named Lambā, the second named Vilambā, the third named Kūṭadantī, the fourth named Puṣpadantī, the fifth named Kṛṣṇadantī, the sixth named Bahukeśī, the seventh named Anivartitā, the eighth named Mālādhārī, the ninth named Kuntī, and the tenth named Sarvasattvojohārī.

These ten rākṣasīs, together with Hārītī and her children and attendants, went to where the Buddha was, and with one voice addressed the Buddha:

“World-Honored One! We too wish to protect those who read, recite, receive, and uphold the Lotus Sūtra, eliminating their misfortunes. If someone seeks the Dharma teacher’s shortcomings, we will cause them not to gain an opening.”

Immediately before the Buddha they spoke the mantra:

ITI ME ITI ME ITI ME ITI ME ITI ME NIME NIME NIME NIME NIME RUHE RUHE RUHE RUHE RUHE STUHE STUHE STUHE STUHE STUHE SVĀHĀ

“Rather climb upon my head than torment the Dharma teacher!

Whether yakṣas, rākṣasas, hungry ghosts, pūtanas, kṛtyas, vetālas, skandas, unmādas, apasmāras, yakṣa-kṛtyas, or human-kṛtyas;

Whether fevers of one day, two days, three days, four days, up to seven days, or constant fevers;

Whether in male form, female form, boy form, or girl form, down to within dreams—none will torment them!”

Immediately before the Buddha they spoke gāthās:

If one disobeys our mantra,

Disturbing the expounder of Dharma,

Their head will break into seven pieces,

Like the branches of the arjaka tree.

Like the sin of killing parents,

Or the misfortune of pressing oil,

Deceiving people with scales and measures,

Or Devadatta’s sin of breaking the Saṅgha:

Those who offend this Dharma teacher,

Will incur misfortunes such as these!

The rākṣasīs, having spoken these gāthās, addressed the Buddha: “World-Honored One! We ourselves will personally protect those who receive, uphold, read, recite, and practice this scripture, causing them to attain peace and security, free of misfortunes, dispelling all poisons.”

The Buddha addressed the rākṣasīs: “Excellent, excellent! That you can protect even those who uphold merely the name of the Lotus, your blessings are beyond measure; how much more so protecting those who fully receive and uphold it, making offerings to the scriptural scrolls—flowers, incense, necklaces, powdered incense, paste incense, burning incense, banners, canopies, music; lighting various lamps: ghee lamps, oil lamps, scented oil lamps, sumanā flower oil lamps, campaka flower oil lamps, vārṣikā flower oil lamps, and utpala flower oil lamps, hundreds of thousands of kinds of offerings like these! Kuntī! You and your attendants should protect such Dharma teachers as these.”

When this Dhāraṇī Chapter was expounded, sixty-eight thousand people attained the forbearance of non-arising dharmas.

← Capítulo anteriorPróximo capítulo →

Capítulo XXVII

As ações passadas do rei Śubhavyūha

Este capítulo ainda está em tradução para o português; é exibido provisoriamente em inglês.

At that time, the Buddha addressed the great assembly: “In the ancient past, immeasurable, boundless, inconceivable incalculable kalpas ago, there was a Buddha named Jaladharagarjitasvaranakṣatrarājasaṃkusumitābhijña Tathāgata, Arhat, Samyaksambuddha. His land was named Prabhāsa-maṇḍita, and his kalpa was named Priyadarśana.

Within the Dharma of that Buddha, there was a king named Śubhavyūha. The king’s consort was named Vimaladattā. He had two sons: one named Vimalagarbha, the second named Vimalanetra.

These two sons possessed great spiritual powers, merit, and wisdom, having long cultivated the path practiced by Bodhisattvas: namely, dāna-pāramitā, śīla-pāramitā, kṣānti-pāramitā, vīrya-pāramitā, dhyāna-pāramitā, prajñā-pāramitā, upāya-pāramitā, love, compassion, joy, and equanimity, down to the thirty-seven factors of awakening, all clearly penetrated and understood. Furthermore, they had attained the Bodhisattva’s Pure samādhi, Constellation samādhi, Pure Light samādhi, Pure Color samādhi, Pure Illumination samādhi, Long Adornment samādhi, and Great Majestic Virtue Treasury samādhi, thoroughly penetrating all these samādhis.

At that time, that Buddha, wishing to guide King Śubhavyūha and out of compassion for wandering beings, expounded this Lotus Sūtra.

Then the two sons, Vimalagarbha and Vimalanetra, went to their mother, joined their palms, and said: ‘We pray our mother will go to where Jaladharagarjitasvaranakṣatrarājasaṃkusumitābhijña Buddha is; we too will follow in attendance, to draw near, make offerings, and bow down. For what reason? This Buddha expounds the Lotus Sūtra amidst all assemblies of devas and humans; it is fitting to listen and receive it.’

The mother said to her sons: ‘Your father believes and accepts non-Buddhist paths, deeply attached to brāhmaṇa dharmas. You should go speak to your father, so that we may go together.’

Vimalagarbha and Vimalanetra joined their palms and said to their mother: ‘We are sons of the Dharma King, yet born into this household of false views.’

The mother said to her sons: ‘You should feel concern for your father, and manifest spiritual transformations for him; if he sees them, his heart will certainly become pure, and perhaps he will permit us to go to the Buddha’s abode.’

Thereupon the two sons, mindful of their father, sprang into open space to the height of seven tāla trees, and displayed various spiritual transformations: walking, standing, sitting, and lying down in open space; emitting water from the upper body and fire from the lower body; emitting water from the lower body and fire from the upper body; manifesting huge bodies filling open space, and then appearing small; appearing small, and then appearing large; vanishing in open space, and suddenly appearing on the ground; entering the ground as if into water, and walking on water as if on dry ground.

Displaying various spiritual transformations such as these, they caused their royal father’s heart to become pure with faith and understanding. At that time, the father, seeing his sons possess such spiritual powers, rejoiced greatly, having attained what he had never had before, joined his palms toward his sons, and said: ‘Who is your teacher? Whose disciples are you?’

The two sons said: ‘Great King! That Jaladharagarjitasvaranakṣatrarājasaṃkusumitābhijña Buddha is now seated upon the Dharma seat beneath the seven-jeweled Bodhi tree, widely expounding the Lotus Sūtra amidst assemblies of devas and humans of all the world; he is our teacher, and we are his disciples.’

The father said to his sons: ‘I now also wish to see your teacher; we may go together.’

Thereupon the two sons descended from open space, went to their mother, joined their palms, and said: ‘Our royal father has now attained faith and understanding, capable of generating the mind of anuttarasamyaksambodhi. We have performed Buddha activities for our father; we pray our mother will grant permission for us to leave home and cultivate the path under that Buddha.’

Then the two sons, wishing to restate their intention, spoke to their mother in gāthās:

Pray let our mother release us,

To leave home and become śramaṇas;

The Buddhas are exceedingly difficult to encounter,

We will follow the Buddha to learn.

Like the uḍumbara flower,

Encountering a Buddha is more difficult than this;

Escaping tribulations is also difficult;

Pray permit us to leave home!

The mother immediately said: ‘I permit you to leave home. For what reason? Because the Buddha is difficult to encounter.’

Thereupon the two sons said to their parents: ‘Excellent, father and mother! Pray go in due time to where Jaladharagarjitasvaranakṣatrarājasaṃkusumitābhijña Buddha is, to draw near and make offerings. For what reason? The Buddha is difficult to encounter, like the uḍumbara flower, and like a one-eyed turtle encountering a hole in a floating log. Yet our past merit is deep and heavy, born to encounter the Buddha-Dharma; therefore our parents should permit us to leave home. For what reason? The Buddhas are difficult to encounter, and the time is difficult to meet.’

At that time, eighty-four thousand people in King Śubhavyūha’s inner palace were all capable of receiving and upholding this Lotus Sūtra. Vimalanetra Bodhisattva had long penetrated the Lotus of Dharma samādhi; Vimalagarbha Bodhisattva had throughout immeasurable hundreds of thousands of millions of kalpas penetrated the samādhi of Abandoning Evil Destinies, wishing to cause all wandering beings to abandon evil destinies. The king’s consort had attained the samādhi of the Gathering of Buddhas, able to know the secret treasuries of the Buddhas.

The two sons, through the power of expedient means, skillfully transformed their father in this manner, causing his heart to believe, understand, and delight in the Buddha-Dharma.

Thereupon King Śubhavyūha, together with his ministers and attendants; Queen Vimaladattā, together with the court ladies of the inner palace; and the king’s two sons, together with forty-two thousand people, all went together to where the Buddha was. Having arrived, they bowed with heads touching his feet, circumambulated the Buddha three times, and withdrew to stand on one side.

Then that Buddha expounded the Dharma for the king, instructing, benefiting, and delighting him. The king was exceedingly glad.

Then King Śubhavyūha and his queen unfastened pearl necklaces from their necks worth hundreds of thousands, scattering them over the Buddha; in open space they transformed into a four-pillared jewel tower, within which was a great precious couch spread with hundreds of thousands of heavenly robes, upon which sat a Buddha cross-legged, emitting great light.

Then King Śubhavyūha thought: ‘The Buddha’s body is rare, upright, extraordinary, accomplishing foremost subtle color.’

Then Jaladharagarjitasvaranakṣatrarājasaṃkusumitābhijña Buddha addressed the fourfold assembly: ‘Do you see this King Śubhavyūha standing with joined palms before me? This king, within my Dharma, will become a bhikṣu, diligently cultivating and aiding the Buddha path, and will become a Buddha named Śālendrarāja, his land named Prabhāsa, and his kalpa named Mahāprabhāvarāja. That Śālendrarāja Buddha will have an immeasurable assembly of Bodhisattvas and immeasurable Śrāvakas, his land level and flat, his merits like this.’

The king immediately entrusted his kingdom to his younger brother, and together with his queen, two sons, and retinues, left home to cultivate the path within the Buddha-Dharma.

Having left home, throughout eighty-four thousand years, the king constantly exerted diligence and vigor in practicing the Lotus Sūtra of the Wonderful Dharma. After this had passed, he attained the samādhi of All Pure Virtue Adornments, immediately ascended into open space to the height of seven tāla trees, and addressed the Buddha:

‘World-Honored One! These two sons of mine have already performed Buddha activities, through spiritual transformations turning my false mind, enabling me to abide peacefully within the Buddha-Dharma and behold the World-Honored One. These two sons are my good friends; wishing to awaken my wholesome roots of past lives and benefit me, they were born into my household.’

Then Jaladharagarjitasvaranakṣatrarājasaṃkusumitābhijña Buddha addressed King Śubhavyūha: ‘Thus it is, thus it is! Just as you have said. If a good man or good woman plants roots of virtue, life after life they attain good friends; their good friends can perform Buddha activities, instructing, benefiting, and delighting them, causing them to enter anuttarasamyaksambodhi.

Great King! You should know that a good friend is a great causal condition: that is to say, guiding and leading so that one attains beholding the Buddha and generating the mind of anuttarasamyaksambodhi.

Great King! Do you see these two sons? These two sons have already made offerings to Buddhas equal to sixty-five hundred thousand billion inconceivable numbers of Ganges sands, drawing near and revering them, receiving and upholding the Lotus Sūtra under those Buddhas, pitying wandering beings of false views and causing them to abide in right view.’

King Śubhavyūha immediately descended from open space and addressed the Buddha: ‘World-Honored One! The Tathāgata is exceedingly rare; through merit and wisdom, the flesh-topknot on his crown shines brightly, his eyes are long, broad, and deep blue, the curl of hair between his eyebrows is white as a gleaming moon, his teeth are white, even, and closely set, constantly radiant, and his lips are red and beautiful like the bimba fruit.’

Then King Śubhavyūha, having praised the Buddha’s immeasurable hundreds of thousands of millions of merits, before the Tathāgata single-mindedly joined his palms and addressed the Buddha again: ‘World-Honored One! It is unprecedented. The Tathāgata’s Dharma is fully endowed with inconceivable, subtle merits; the conduct taught by his precepts brings peace and swift goodness. From this day forward, I will no longer follow the impulses of my own mind, nor give rise to false views, arrogance, anger, or evil thoughts.’ Having spoken these words, he bowed to the Buddha and departed.”

The Buddha addressed the great assembly: “What do you think: could King Śubhavyūha have been someone else? It was Padmaśrī Bodhisattva himself. Queen Vimaladattā was Vairocanaraśmipratimaṇḍitadhvajā Bodhisattva, who now stands before the Buddha, born in that realm out of compassion for King Śubhavyūha and his retinues.

The two sons were Bhaiṣajyarāja Bodhisattva and Bhaiṣajyasamudgata Bodhisattva. These Bodhisattvas Bhaiṣajyarāja and Bhaiṣajyasamudgata have accomplished such great merits, having planted roots of virtue under immeasurable hundreds of thousands of millions of Buddhas, accomplishing inconceivable wholesome merits. If someone knows the names of these two Bodhisattvas, devas and humans of all the world should bow down to them in homage.”

When the Buddha expounded this Former activities of King Śubhavyūha Chapter, eighty-four thousand persons departed from dust and defilement, attaining the pure Dharma eye amidst all dharmas.

Capítulo XXVIII

A exortação do bodhisattva Samantabhadra

Este capítulo ainda está em tradução para o português; é exibido provisoriamente em inglês.

At that time, Samantabhadra Bodhisattva, through sovereign spiritual powers, majestic virtue, and fame, together with an immeasurable, boundless, countless assembly of great Bodhisattvas, arrived from the eastern direction. The lands they passed through universally shook, raining jewel lotus flowers, and producing immeasurable hundreds of thousands of millions of varieties of music.

Furthermore, surrounded by an assembly of countless devas, Nagas, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kiṃnaras, mahoragas, humans, and non-humans, each displaying majestic virtue and spiritual powers, he arrived at Mount Gṛdhrakūṭa in the Sahā world. Bowing with his head touching the feet of Śākyamuni Buddha, he circumambulated him seven times to the right, and addressed the Buddha:

“World-Honored One! In the land of Ratnatejobhyudgatarāja Buddha, from afar I heard the Lotus Sūtra being expounded in this Sahā world, and together with an immeasurable, boundless assembly of hundreds of thousands of millions of Bodhisattvas have come to listen and receive it. We only pray that the World-Honored One will expound it for us. If good men and good women, after the Tathāgata’s extinction, wish to attain this Lotus Sūtra, how can they attain it?”

The Buddha addressed Samantabhadra Bodhisattva: “If a good man or good woman accomplishes four dharmas, after the Tathāgata’s extinction they will attain this Lotus Sūtra: first, being guarded in the mindfulness of the Buddhas; second, planting roots of virtue; third, entering the assembly of right determination; fourth, generating the mind to save all wandering beings. If a good man or good woman accomplishes these four dharmas, after the Tathāgata’s extinction they will certainly attain this scripture.”

Then Samantabhadra Bodhisattva addressed the Buddha: “World-Honored One! In the final five hundred years of the turbid, evil world, if there are those who receive and uphold this scripture, I will guard and protect them, eliminate their misfortunes, cause them to attain peace and security, and ensure that none who seek their shortcomings gain an opening:

Whether Maras, Mara sons, Mara daughters, Mara subjects, those possessed by Maras, yakṣas, rākṣasas, kumbhāṇḍas, piśācas, kṛtyas, pūtanas, or vetālas—all who torment people will gain no opening.

If this person, walking or standing, reads and recites this scripture, I at that time, riding a six-tusked white elephant king together with an assembly of great Bodhisattvas, will go to where they are, manifest my body, make offerings, protect them, comfort their minds, and make offerings to the Lotus Sūtra.

If this person sits and contemplates this scripture, I will again ride the white elephant king and manifest before them. If that person forgets a single phrase or a single verse of the Lotus Sūtra, I will teach them, read and recite it together with them, and restore their fluency.

At that time, those who receive, uphold, read, and recite the Lotus Sūtra will behold my body, rejoice greatly, and redouble their vigor; because of seeing me, they will immediately attain samādhis and dhāraṇīs: named the Rotating dhāraṇī, the Hundreds of Thousands of Millions of Rotations dhāraṇī, and the Dharma Sound Expedient Means dhāraṇī—attaining dhāraṇīs such as these.

World-Honored One! If in future worlds during the final five hundred years of the turbid, evil world, bhikṣus, bhikṣuṇīs, upāsakas, or upāsikās who seek, receive, uphold, read, recite, and copy, wishing to cultivate this Lotus Sūtra, for three times seven days they should be single-mindedly vigorous.

When three times seven days are fulfilled, I will ride a six-tusked white elephant, surrounded by immeasurable Bodhisattvas, and in a body that all wandering beings delight in seeing, manifest before that person to expound the Dharma, instructing, benefiting, and delighting them, and will also bestow upon them a dhāraṇī mantra. Because of attaining this dhāraṇī, no non-human can destroy them, nor can they be seduced by women; I myself will also constantly protect this person. We only pray that the World-Honored One will permit me to speak this dhāraṇī mantra.”

Immediately before the Buddha he spoke the mantra:

ADAṆḌE DAṆḌAPATI DAṆḌĀVARTANI DAṆḌAKUŚALE DAṆḌASUDHĀRI SUDHĀRI SUDHĀRAPATI BUDDHAPAŚYANE SARVADHĀRAṆĪ-ĀVARTANI SARVABHĀṢĀ-ĀVARTANI SU-ĀVARTANI SAṄGHAPARĪKṢAṆI SAṄGHANIRGHĀTANI ASAMPI SAṄGHAPAGATE TI-ADHVA-SAṄGATULYA-ARATE-PRĀPTE SARVASAṄGĀ-SAMATIKRĀNTE SARVADHARMA-SUPARĪKṢITE SARVASATTVARUTAKAUŚALYĀNUGATE SIṂHAVIKRĪḌITE SVĀHĀ

“World-Honored One! If there are Bodhisattvas who hear this dhāraṇī, they should know that it is the spiritual power of Samantabhadra. If the Lotus Sūtra travels through Jambudvīpa and there are those who uphold it, they should think: ‘This is entirely through the majestic spiritual power of Samantabhadra.’

If someone receives, upholds, reads, recites, properly remembers, understands its meaning, and practices as taught, you should know that this person practices the conduct of Samantabhadra, has deeply planted roots of virtue under immeasurable, boundless Buddhas, and their head is rubbed by the hands of the Tathāgatas.

If they merely copy it, when this person’s life ends, they will be reborn in the Trāyastriṃśa Heaven; at that time, eighty-four thousand heavenly maidens making music will come to welcome them, and that person, wearing a crown of the seven treasures, will delight in entertainment amidst the maidens; how much more so receiving, upholding, reading, reciting, properly remembering, understanding its meaning, and practicing as taught!

If someone receives, upholds, reads, recites, and understands its meaning, when their life ends, a thousand Buddhas will extend their hands, so that they will not fear nor fall into evil destinies, immediately proceeding to Tuṣita Heaven to where Maitreya Bodhisattva is. Maitreya Bodhisattva, endowed with the thirty-two marks, is surrounded by an assembly of great Bodhisattvas and has hundreds of thousands of millions of heavenly maidens as attendants; being born in their midst, they possess such merits and benefits as these.

Therefore, those who possess wisdom should single-mindedly copy it themselves or cause others to copy it, receive, uphold, read, recite, properly remember, and practice it as taught.

World-Honored One! I now, through spiritual powers, protect this scripture; after the Tathāgata’s extinction, within Jambudvīpa I will cause it to be widely propagated, never allowing it to be cut off.”

Then Śākyamuni Buddha praised: “Excellent, excellent, Samantabhadra! That you are able to protect and aid this scripture, bringing peace, joy, and benefit to many wandering beings. You have already accomplished inconceivable merits, deep and great compassion; from the distant past onward, you generated the resolve for anuttarasamyaksambodhi, and are able to make such vows of spiritual powers to protect this scripture. I, through spiritual powers, will protect those who can receive and uphold the name of Samantabhadra Bodhisattva.

Samantabhadra! If someone receives, upholds, reads, recites, properly remembers, cultivates, and copies this Lotus Sūtra, you should know that this person beholds Śākyamuni Buddha, as if hearing this scripture directly from the Buddha’s mouth; you should know that this person makes offerings to Śākyamuni Buddha; you should know that for this person, the Buddha praises: ‘Excellent!’; you should know that this person’s head is rubbed by the hand of Śākyamuni Buddha; you should know that this person is covered by the robe of Śākyamuni Buddha.

Such a person as this will no longer be greedily attached to worldly pleasures, will not favor non-Buddhist scriptures or writings, and will not delight in drawing close to such people or evil persons—such as butchers, those who raise pigs, sheep, chickens, and dogs, hunters, or those who peddle female charms.

This person’s mind is upright and direct, with proper mindfulness, possessing the power of merit and virtue. This person is not tormented by the three poisons, nor tormented by envy, self-grasping pride, perverted pride, or arrogant pride. This person has few desires, knows contentment, and is able to cultivate the practices of Samantabhadra.

Samantabhadra! After the Tathāgata’s extinction, in the final five hundred years, if someone sees one who receives, upholds, reads, and recites the Lotus Sūtra, they should think: ‘This person before long will proceed to the place of awakening, shatter the hosts of Maras, attain anuttarasamyaksambodhi, turn the wheel of Dharma, strike the Dharma drum, blow the Dharma conch, rain the Dharma rain, and sit upon the lion Dharma seat amidst the great assembly of devas and humans.’

Samantabhadra! If in future worlds someone receives, upholds, reads, and recites this scripture, this person will no longer crave clothing, bedding, food, drink, or livelihoods; what they wish will not be in vain, and in this present life they will attain karmic retributions of blessing.

If someone slights and defames them, saying: ‘You are a mad person, vainly performing these practices, ultimately obtaining nothing’; for such sinful retribution, life after life they will have no eyes.

If someone makes offerings and praises them, in this present life they will attain present visible fruits.

If someone sees an upholder of this scripture and proclaims their faults and evils, whether real or unreal, this person in this present life will contract white leprosy.

If someone derisively laughs at them, life after life their teeth will be sparse and missing, with ugly lips, flat nose, twisted limbs, squinting eyes, a stinking body, covered in foul sores, pus, blood, dropsy, and shortness of breath, suffering from various severe illnesses.

Therefore, Samantabhadra! If you see someone who upholds this scripture, you should rise and welcome them from afar, showing reverence as if to the Buddha!”

When this Encouragement of Bodhisattva Samantabhadra Chapter was expounded, Bodhisattvas equal to the sands of the Ganges attained hundreds of thousands of millions of rotating dhāraṇīs; Bodhisattvas equal to the dust motes of a great three-thousand-fold worlds fully perfected the path of Samantabhadra.

When the Buddha expounded this scripture, Samantabhadra and the Bodhisattvas, Śāriputra and the Śrāvakas, as well as devas, Nagas, humans, and non-humans, the entire great assembly, rejoiced greatly, received and upheld the Buddha’s words, bowed in reverence, and departed.

This is the Sūtra of the Lotus Flower of the Wonderful Dharma.

Dedicatória

Eu, o Acharya Vajraprajna, traduzi esta profunda segunda parte, a principal, do compêndio do Sutra de Lótus. Porque ela brilha como a lua que reflete uma luz suave e curativa, dedico esta tradução à longa vida e aos dias de alegria sem fim de Dawa. Que ela seja fonte de todos os Brahmaviharas! Sarva Mangalam!

Fim do sūtra
